# Volt build queue

The work list the scheduled **Volt build queue** task runs through, one item
per run: build it, validate it, commit, cut a release, record the result here
and in `VOLT_BUILD_STATUS.md`, then stop. The next run takes the next item.
How a run works, and how to validate, is in `BUILD_RUNBOOK.md`.

**Goal of the queue:** every key element in place and tested end to end on a
single Mac server, then ready to deploy to AWS with the Terraform in
`deploy/terraform` and to exercise the cloud-specific functions that make Volt
cost effective (ECS launcher, per-run S3 prefixes, retention and archive,
consolidation, edge rights, scaling).

**The key driver is cost** (Christian, 27 Oct): Volt is offered as a SaaS
service, so every item keeps the fleet as small and cheap as the work allows.
Spin up the right instance type for each job, no bigger; no large GPU units.
Before a stream is allocated, project its CPU, memory, GPU, bandwidth and
storage from its bitrates and the number of renditions, and put it on a
running server that can take it until a tolerance (CPU ceiling, or the
server's validated bandwidth allowance) would be passed; only then start a
new server. Consolidate automatically to keep servers full. Learn from what
actually runs, so the projections get more accurate over time.

**Statuses:** `todo` · `doing (since <ISO time>, run <id>)` · `done (v0.27.0, <date>)` ·
`skipped: <why>` · `needs input: <question for Christian>` · `blocked: <what>`.
A run takes the FIRST `todo` item. Order is deliberate; do not reorder without
a reason written in the item.

Created 22 Oct 2026 from Christian's list, triaged against the goal.

---

| 1.5 | **Smart render seam fix.** Byte-concat the TS parts and regenerate timestamps so the join is seamless (concat demuxer offsets by container duration while the picture ends earlier) | done (v0.5.1, 22 Oct 2026) | Encoder test: render a clip across a seam with ffmpeg test sources, assert no timestamp gap or overlap (ffprobe packet PTS continuity) | **Fixed.** Cause: the concat demuxer started each part at the previous file's container duration, which runs to the end of the AAC, not of the picture (the copied middle: 12.000 s of video, 12.022 s of file), so the tail landed 23 ms late at 25 fps (30 ms at 50 fps with B-frames). Fix in `services/clips/extract.ts`: the join list now gives each part a `duration` measured from its picture (`concatListByPicture`: video start and length from ffprobe, cross-checked against frames times frame time, refusing a part that does not add up), and the smart render's runtime verify now also checks every frame step for a gap or overlap (`seamFault`, packet timestamps, no decode), so a seam error falls back to the full render instead of shipping. Chose the demuxer's own `duration` directive over a raw Annex-B byte concat with regenerated timestamps: same result, and a raw H.264 stream loses B-frame presentation order, which the live encoder uses. **Tests:** `test/clip-smart-render.check.ts` **35/35** (was 23/24; 11 new: no gap or overlap in either render, one frame step across head and tail seams, a planted 23 ms gap is caught, and a second source at 50 fps with B-frames cut at 1.3 s to 14.7 s matching the full render frame for frame, 670 frames). Against the code before the fix: 29/35, 6 failing (tail seam at frame 338, 23.2 ms gap; 50 fps gap of 30.2 ms at frame 136, one frame short). Full `node scripts/check.js`: **183/183 suites, 5559 assertions, 0 failed** (first all-green full run; Postgres 16, own DB per suite, cloud container with the sibling folders present). Neighbours: clip-extract 33/33, ingest-extract 7/7, scheduled-clips 15/15, vod-extractor 16/16, clip-provenance ok. Typechecks clean on the device. **Not changed:** smart render is still opt-in (`smartRender: true`) and no caller turns it on, so clips still use the full render. **Christian:** say if clip extraction should use smart render by default now that the suite is green (about 2x faster on the fixture, falls back to the full render on any doubt). |
| 1.6 | **SCTE-35 / emsg ad signalling (video).** DATERANGE is in; add SCTE-35 cues (EXT-X-DATERANGE SCTE35-OUT/IN for HLS, emsg scte35 for DASH) from ad breaks | done (v0.6.0, 22 Oct 2026) | Playlist and segment tests: cues present at the break times, valid SCTE-35 binary, players ignore them safely | **Built.** FAST channels already had SCTE-35; live channels and events did not (their breaks, from Go to break, the API or a FAST break, reached the encoder only as our own ad trigger). Now every break carries it. **Control plane** (`services/graphicTriggers.ts`): `breakCue()` builds the splice_insert pair with the FAST builder (`services/fast/scte35.ts`), splice immediate with break duration and auto return (a live stream's PTS comes from the source and the control plane never sees it, so the carrier gives the moment: START-DATE in HLS, presentationTime in DASH); the splice_event_id is the break's own `scte_event_id` when it has one, else the same stable hash FAST uses. The break's DATERANGE gains `PLANNED-DURATION` and `SCTE35-OUT` (unquoted hex, RFC 8216 4.3.2.7.1); the encoder poll (`GET /api/streams/:id/timed-events`) carries the pair and the DASH event (`dashScte35`). **Encoder** (`volt-encoder/bin/timed_events.py`): `place_cues()` writes `EXT-X-CUE-OUT` / `CUE-OUT-CONT` (elapsed time) / `CUE-IN` on the segments a break covers (a segment is in the break when its midpoint is; a window opening mid-break starts with CUE-OUT-CONT); the MPD gets an EventStream `urn:scte:scte35:2014:xml+bin` with the cue base64 in Signal/Binary at the break start, marked `value="com.boomlabs.volt"` so a rewrite replaces only ours and a SCTE-35 stream the packager passed through is left alone. Fails open as before. **Tests:** new `test/live-scte35.check.ts` **43/43** (bytes decode with valid CRC, splice_insert, immediate, duration, auto return, event id; tag grammar; DASH bytes; the Volt SDK still reads one ad break; then end to end: a `stream_breaks` row, the real encoder poll, the real encoder placement on a live playlist and MPD written around now, cues read back at the break time and decoded; a cancelled break gets no cue). Against the code before this change it stops at its first assertion. `test_timed_events.py` **65/65** (was 34; 31 new on the captured manifests: cue placement, mid-break join, idempotence, a break crossing Periods gets one out, a passed-through SCTE-35 stream untouched, a malformed cue not written); against the old encoder code 8 fail and the suite stops. Full `node scripts/check.js`: **181/184 suites, 5546 assertions passed, 19 failed** in one run; live-record failed only because the container copy had no volt-web node_modules (with them 24/24), recordings 17/35 in the full run and 35/35 alone (the known shared path), fast 44/45 (`the programme on air is left alone`, fails the same on the code before this change). Neighbours: graphic-triggers 47/47, player-triggers 38/38, timed-metadata 37/37, fast-ads 48/48, fast-graphics 15/15, fast-lineup 56/56. Typechecks clean on the device. **Players** (Chromium, VP9 test segments, the encoder's own output, break 30 s): hls.js 1.6.17 parses the DATERANGE as valid with SCTE35-OUT and PLANNED-DURATION and sees the 8 cue lines, plays on; Shaka 4.16 surfaces SCTE35-OUT on HLS at 20 to 50 s and a `urn:scte:scte35` timeline region on DASH at 20 to 50 s, plays both; dash.js 4.7.4 dispatches the SCTE-35 event (presentation time 20, duration 30, payload present) and plays through the break; no player or page errors. **Not yet:** in-band `emsg` SCTE-35 inside the DASH segments (the MPD EventStream carries the same cue, which is what dash.js, Shaka and DASH ad inserters read; in-band would need the packager to write it); the local `/origin` path on the Mac adds the DATERANGE with SCTE35-OUT but not the CUE lines (the encoder's delivered copies have both); reading SCTE-35 from a live source is still to do. Not run against the real Shaka packager and encoder on the Mac: on the Mac, start a channel, Go to break, and check `stream_*_cc.m3u8` for CUE-OUT and `manifest_cc.mpd` for the scte35 EventStream. |
| 1.7 | **ID3-in-emsg now-playing (audio).** Its own path and test, not folded into the video work | done (v0.7.0, 23 Oct 2026) | Radio HLS/DASH segments carry ID3 TIT2/TPE1 timed to the now-playing changes; test with a synthetic ICY source | **Built.** A station's title changes now travel inside its HLS and DASH audio as ID3 in `emsg` (scheme `https://aomedia.org/emsg/ID3`, emsg version 1, ID3v2.4 TIT2 title and TPE1 artist, UTF-8). **Control plane** (`services/radio/nowPlayingTimeline.ts`): the timed-events poll the radio container already makes now carries `now_playing` for radio stations only: each change in the content log the ICY reader writes (start, id, title, artist), plus the one in force when the window opens; a station with no ICY titles gets its schedule. A song (the ICY reader's class) is split "Artist - Title" into TPE1 and TIT2; other titles go out whole; an empty title (a break) shows the station name. **Encoder** (`volt-encoder/bin/id3_emsg.py`, called from `radio.py` delivered copies): a segment that a change falls in, or the title in force every 30 s for listeners who join mid-song, is copied to `audio/id3/` with the emsg placed before its moof (the sidx first_offset moved by the same amount), timed on the track's own timeline from the segment's PROGRAM-DATE-TIME; `index_cc.m3u8` points that segment at the copy. DASH names segments by number, so `dash/id3/` holds the whole rolling window (hard links where there is no tag) and `manifest_cc.mpd` names it and declares the InbandEventStream. ffmpeg's own files, which recordings and clipping read, are never touched; any odd segment or MPD fails open to ffmpeg's file. A change the encoder learns after its segment went out lands at the start of the next one: never early, at most a segment plus a poll late. `Dockerfile.radio` now copies `id3_emsg.py` (and a test fails if a module radio.py imports is not copied). **Tests:** new `test/radio-id3.check.ts` **36/36**, end to end with a synthetic ICY source (`icecast-sim.js`), the real ICY reader, the timed-events endpoint and the real `radio.py` in local mode for 40 s: 7 tags in HLS and DASH, every one a title from the log with the artist split as logged, 0.3 to 1.1 s after its change, all 5 changes in the window carried, ffmpeg's segments and playlists byte-identical and without emsg, tagged copies' audio identical; against the code before this change 8 of 36 fail. New `test_id3_emsg.py` **51/51** (ID3 and emsg bytes, sidx offset, the plan for each segment including a late change and the repeat, real ffmpeg HLS and DASH output decoding to the same audio, fail-open cases, the Dockerfile check); it cannot import without the change. Neighbours: test_radio_triggers.py 22/22, test_timed_events.py 65/65, radio.check 41/41, radio-container 23/23, radio-outputs 18/18, now-playing 32/32, timed-metadata 37/37, live-scte35 43/43. Full `node scripts/check.js`: **184/185 suites, 5646 assertions passed, 16 failed**, all in recordings during the full run (35/35 alone, the known shared path). Typechecks clean on the device. **Players** (Chromium, the suite's own 40 s run): hls.js 1.6.17 reports all 7 ID3 tags at their media times on HLS; Shaka 4.16 all of them on DASH, and on HLS once `mediaSource.dispatchAllEmsgBoxes` is on (Shaka applies the DASH rule of ignoring emsg the manifest does not declare, and HLS cannot declare it); dash.js 4.7.4 dispatches all 7 on DASH; all play through with no errors. **Not yet:** the Volt player SDK still takes now-playing from its poll, not from these tags; precision is the content log's (the ICY reader's own connection), within a segment plus a poll (5 s); on the Mac, start a station on an Icecast source and check `audio/id3/` and `dash/id3/` fill and that Safari shows the title. |
| 1.8 | **Graphics: live updates over SSE.** Live control currently polls every second; push state changes on the existing status stream | done (v0.8.0, 23 Oct 2026) | Check suite: an air/state change reaches a subscriber in under 1 s; live control drops the poll | **Built.** Every graphics state change is announced on the admin status stream (`GET /api/status/stream`) as a `gfx` frame: which channel or event, why (values, air, rundown, feed, assignment, setup, tick) and the assignment, never the values (a page re-reads with its own credential, as with `record` frames). **Write paths** (`services/graphics/runtime.ts`, `modules/graphics.ts`, new `services/graphics/livePush.ts`): values, take on and off, the off timer, a pushed or polled feed (every stream bound to it), rundown add, edit, reorder, take, take off, skip, an assignment or setup edit (every stream using it), a data source's stale rule; each announced after its transaction commits. **Time path:** the leader's one-second graphics tick now resolves each live stream's graphics and announces the streams whose on/off, values or staleness moved with no write (a rule graphic at a programme boundary, a feed going stale); first sight only records. **Who gets them:** the stream now also opens for a role with Graphics alone (it receives only `gfx` frames, no status snapshots); a role without Graphics receives no `gfx` frames (`statusHub.subscribe` takes a per-viewer filter). **Console:** `liveStatus.ts` routes `gfx` frames to their own keys (`gfx:channel:<id>`, so pages that refetch a channel on status news are not woken by score changes); new `graphics/useGfxLive.ts` re-reads on a frame or a reconnect, folds frames within 120 ms, reads every 3 s only while the stream is down and every 30 s as a safety net. Live control drops its 1 s poll, the running event rundown its 2 s poll, the channel graphics tab its 5 s poll. **Tests:** new `test/graphics-live-push.check.ts` **32/32** (frames for values 6 ms, take on 8 ms, take off 6 ms, feed push 5 ms, take next 6 ms after the API call, bar 1000 ms; the off timer, setup edits on a channel and an event, a rule going off at a programme boundary through the watch; nothing for an unchanged value or payload; no values in the frame; another account gets nothing; Graphics-only and Live-only roles get only their frames; a role with neither 403; the three consoles no longer poll). | 1.9 | **Graphics: overlays in the Volt player.** Player loads the assigned setup's published version from `/gfx/<version>/` in a sandboxed layer and follows live state | done (v0.9.0, 23 Oct 2026) | Player test with a sample template: shows, updates, hides; CSP sandbox kept | **Mostly built on 20 Oct; this run tested it end to end and fixed two faults the test found.** The overlay itself (`volt-player/src/sdk/overlay.ts`, the public feed `GET /api/player/graphics`, sandboxed `/gfx/<version>/`) was already in place with only its pure parts under test. **Fixed:** (1) a setup trying out an unpublished draft was sent to viewers' players (the feed did not look at the version's status, so a draft that can still change or carry check errors reached air); the feed now lists published versions only (`services/graphics/playerFeed.ts`), and such a setup is not drawn in players until it is moved to a published version. (2) `VoltPlayer.create()` with no `skin` (documented default: video) marked the player `data-skin="custom"`, so the video skin's styles never applied: a stage 0 px high, no picture and an overlay layer left unscaled at 1920x1080 (`sdk/player.ts`; the declarative embed was not affected, it names `video`). **Tests:** new `test/player-overlay-browser.check.ts` **31/31**: a sample template pasted, published, set up and assigned to a live channel through the real API and Postgres; the real SDK bundled from source plays a live VP9 HLS stream with PROGRAM-DATE-TIME in Chromium on another origin; taken on through the API it shows with its values, a value change updates it in place, taken off it hides, each about 8 s after the call (the playhead sits about 6 s behind live and a change is applied when the picture reaches it, as designed); player state names the overlay on screen; the 1920x1080 canvas is scaled into the 16:9 picture; the iframe is `sandbox=allow-scripts` only, served with CSP `sandbox allow-scripts` and `connect-src` its own package, runs at an opaque origin, cannot read cookies or the host page, cannot fetch the host site or the Volt API, can still read its own package; a draft setup taken on air is never in the feed or drawn. Against the code before: 22/31 (9 fail). The browser part skips and says so when playwright-core, ffmpeg with libvpx or volt-player's node_modules are missing. | 1.11 | **Server-side ads: impression and quartile beacons** for stitched breaks, fired server-side, recorded per break | done (v0.11.0, 23 Oct 2026) | Test against a local VAST stub: beacons hit in order (impression, start, quartiles, complete); report shows them | **Built.** The stitcher now reports each paid ad it puts in a viewer's break to the ad server itself: every impression URL (inline and each wrapper), then `creativeView`, `start`, `firstQuartile`, `midpoint`, `thirdQuartile`, `complete` and VAST 4 `progress` offsets. **When** (`services/fast/beacons.ts`, pure): segments are shared CDN files, so the stitcher places the viewer from its own playlist refreshes: end of the newest segment minus the hold back (`HOLD-BACK`, else 3 target durations, the hls.js, Shaka and Safari default), and sends what that position has passed, only for breaks this viewer was actually given (a viewer joining mid-break gets the house fill and reports nothing). A viewer who leaves stops refreshing, so no complete; refreshes that stop for over 30 s skip what fell due meanwhile instead of sending it late. **How** (`services/fast/beaconSender.ts`): each URL is claimed in a new `ad_beacons` table (migration 0078, unique per session, break, ad, event and URL) before the GET, so two API tasks never send one twice; one chain per session keeps the order; `X-Device-IP` and `X-Device-User-Agent` (VAST 4.1 server-side reporting), `[TIMESTAMP]`, `[CACHEBUSTING]`, `[ADPLAYHEAD]`, `[BREAKPOSITION]` filled; 5 s, redirects followed by hand; private addresses refused (`AD_BEACONS_ALLOW_PRIVATE=1` for a local stub only); answer recorded, failures not retried; never on the manifest path (the playlist is answered first). **Report:** `GET /api/fast/channels/:id/ad-report` gains `beacons` (per event the ads that reached it, URLs sent and failed, impressions, completes, completion rate); new `GET /api/fast/channels/:id/breaks/:bid/beacons` (per ad in the pod and event, viewers, sent, failed; latest 100 with the tracker host only); API reference overlay, `docs:routes`, `docs:openapi` regenerated. Console: the Ads tab's Last 7 days card shows Reported to the ad server (impressions, reached halfway, played to the end, completion, failed pixels). **Tests:** new `test/fast-beacons.check.ts` **51/51**: pure scheduling, playhead, due and skipped, macros, address guard; then end to end through the real API and Postgres with a stand-in playout writing a live playlist (2 s segments, 16 s break), a local VAST stub (wrapper plus two inline ads, one tracker redirecting, one answering 500) and three viewers refreshing every second: S1 (video and audio playlists) sends all 16 beacons once, in order, 6.7 to 6.8 s after each moment in the stream, with the device headers and macros; S2 leaving at the first quartile sends no midpoint or complete; S3 joining mid-break sends nothing; 22 rows, the 500 recorded as failed; report 3 impressions, 2 completes, 67 %; per break both ads; another account 404. | 1.14a | **Edge rights variants: the rule reducer and edge decision.** Per EDGE_RIGHTS_VARIANTS.md: reduce a stream's active EPG rights to variants (one per distinct replace timeline, key = hash), publish them in the KeyValueStore entry, and change the viewer-request function to choose allow / block / replace:<key> from country plus a signed rights-class claim in the playback token. Replacement is a slate or a loop from Loops & Fillers; a loop of any length repeats to fill the window and is cut at its end (Christian, 22 Oct). Delivery side only: encoder, segments and recordings untouched | done (no release yet, 23 Oct 2026) | Reducer tests (overlapping AU and NZ windows give two variants; same rules share one); edge function and `edgeRights.ts` parity test on one fixture table; HMAC claim verified and a forged one rejected; fail-open kept; the same decision runs on the Mac in `publicLive.ts` with the development-only test country, rights class and time (EDGE_RIGHTS_VARIANTS.md, On the Mac first) | **Built.** **Reducer** (`services/edgeRights.ts`): `reduceVariants()` / `streamVariants()` work out each audience's replace timeline (every country the windows name plus `*` for any other or unknown country, each also with every rights class a window exempts), interval by interval exactly as the edge decides (first window that applies wins); audiences with the same timeline share one variant, key `v` + 8 hex of its hash. The KeyValueStore value gains `vm` (audience to key, only where the more general entry would differ), an optional 6th window element (exempt classes, from `programmes.metadata.rights_exempt`) and `vs` (variant service running, off until 1.14b via `EDGE_VARIANT_SERVICE=1`); the map is built from exactly the windows that fit the 1 KB. `_rp` is gone. **Decision**: `decideFor` / `variantFor` / `edgeDecision` give allow, block or replace:<key>; a replace with no variant or no variant service is a block. **Claim**: `signRightsClaim` / `verifyRightsClaim`, `<class>.<exp>.<sig>` tied to one stream, HMAC-SHA256 with `EDGE_RIGHTS_CLAIM_KEY` (or a key derived from the JWT secret); the orchestrator's sync publishes it at `k:rc`. **Edge** (`deploy/edge/rights-viewer-request.js`, 6.4 KB): verifies `vrc` from the query or a cookie, rewrites to `/<kind>/<id>/v/<key>/<file>`, and serves a variant asked for by name only to its own audience (no way round a block). **Mac** (`modules/publicLive.ts` `publicRightsDecision`): the public `/live/<id>/` route makes the same decision; outside production `?as=`, `&rc=`, `&at=` and the `X-Volt-Test-Country` / `X-Volt-Test-Rights` headers; answers carry `X-Volt-Rights`, vary by country, and are not shared when per viewer; a replace answers 451 naming the variant until the variant service exists, then 302 to `/live/<id>/v/<key>/`. **Checked:** new `test/edge-variants.check.ts` **62/62** (reducer cases incl. AU then NZ, shared and overlapping windows, allow lists, rights classes; 408 replace decisions each covered by their variant's timeline; 60 windows cut to 1 KB with the map from the published windows only; the real function file beside `edgeRights.ts` over **8840** fixture cases, all equal; claims: app-signed verified by the function, forged, other class, expired, other stream, other key all rejected; handler rewrites, cookie claim, no key, direct variant requests, fail-open; on Postgres with real HTTP: dev `?as=AU` 451 with the AU variant, MY passes, header, `&at=` +90 min replaces NZ, `&rc=premium` exempt, 302 with the variant service on, production ignores `as`, `rc` and the test header, honours a signed cookie or query claim and refuses a forged one). Would fail without the change (no `streamVariants`). `edge-rights` 38/38 (the `_rp` checks now check the variant rewrite), `edge-viewas` 5/5. Full `node scripts/check.js`: 176/199 in the first pass with only volt-app in the copy; with the sibling folders added every one of those passes alone except `live-degraded` (12/17), which fails identically with this item's files put back to HEAD (cause is elsewhere in the uncommitted tree). volt-app typecheck clean on the device. **Left:** how a viewer gets a rights class (needs input, below); the Multiview view-as path `/origin/<id>/as/<cc>/` in `server.ts` still looks for `_rp` files (another session has uncommitted edits in `server.ts`; 1.14c replaces it with the variant path); variant manifests themselves are 1.14b; the Rights preview is 1.14c. Not released: another session's uncommitted CHANGELOG and change notes are still in the tree. |
| 1.14b | **Edge rights variants: the manifest service.** `/live/<id>/v/<key>/<playlist>` builds the variant from the current delivered playlist, substituting the pre-packaged replacement segments (fillerPrep's loop, matched to the ladder) inside each window, with discontinuities at each boundary and loop wrap (HLS) and Periods (DASH); stateless, short cache, Terraform for its CloudFront behaviour with Origin Shield | done (no release yet, 23 Oct 2026) | Playlist tests: a 5 minute loop over a 20 minute window repeats four times and ends exactly at the window end; PROGRAM-DATE-TIME continuous; two variants served at once from one source; recordings and the origin's own playlists byte-identical before and after; a small load test showing origin requests scale with variants, not viewers; on the Mac, two tabs (as=AU, as=MY) play the two versions at once | **Built.** `volt-app/src/services/variantManifest.ts` answers `/live/<id>/v/<key>/<file>` (the Mac: the public route now redirects a replaced viewer there, `?as`, `&rc`, `&at` and a query claim carried along and passed on to every playlist the master lists) and `/channel/<id>/v/<key>/<file>` or `/event/...` (the cloud: the edge function rewrites the viewer's own URL and now picks the `variants` origin by id). It reads the playlist the encoder delivered (disk on the Mac, S3 in the cloud, one read per file per second shared by every variant) and puts the stream's pre-built loop (fillerPrep's, the same package failover uses) in place of the live segments inside each window. **HLS** slot for slot: each replaced slot keeps the live segment's duration and PROGRAM-DATE-TIME, so media sequence and clock are the source's; discontinuity plus the loop's own init at the window start, at every loop wrap and at the window end; the discontinuity sequence is worked out from the windows, so it holds as the playlist slides; subtitles get an empty WebVTT inside the window; an encrypted stream announces the clear loop and its key comes back after. **DASH** as Periods: live Periods cut at the window edges (offset moved so nothing shifts), one Period per pass of the loop from loop time 0, the last cut at the window end, only what is available listed. Each viewer gets their own variant whatever key the path names (a key is a cache name, not a permission), 451 for a block, the normal on-air answers, and 503 instead of the programme when no loop is built or a rendition has no match. On outside production by default (`EDGE_VARIANT_SERVICE=0` turns it off; Terraform sets 1). **Terraform**: `variants` origin (the control plane's load balancer, Origin Shield in the stack's region), `/channel/*/v/*` and `/event/*/v/*` behaviours with the rights function, an origin request policy passing the country and the `vrc` claim, the public path rule on the load balancer, `EDGE_VARIANT_SERVICE`; `terraform validate` passes (Terraform 1.9.8 in the container). **Checked:** new `test/edge-variant-manifests.check.ts` **62/62**: a 5 minute loop over a 20 minute window plays four times in order and stops exactly at the window end, PDT and durations identical to the source, 5 discontinuities at the start, every 75 slots and the end; the discontinuity number of every segment the same across 61 sliding 30 segment playlists with a source discontinuity mixed in; jittered audio off the grid never repeats a loop segment; refusals (no loop, unknown rendition, MPEG-TS); DASH: live, four passes, live again with the right starts, offsets and segment numbers, a live edge inside the window, valid XML; the edge function sends a replaced viewer to the `variants` origin and an allowed one to S3; on Postgres with real HTTP: AU and NZ served two different variants at the same moment from one source, MY the normal stream, MY asking for AU's key gets its own, CN (channel geo) and FJ (blackout) 451 on the variant path, DASH variant valid, `&at=` an hour on gives normal content, off air 404, the CDN-shaped path with the country header, no loop 503, service off 404; **load** with a CDN in front (cache for s-maxage, misses collapsed): 20 viewers 12 service requests and 4 origin reads in 6 s, 1000 viewers 12 and 8; the origin's files byte for byte unchanged. Would fail without the change (no `variantManifest`). Also rendered against the real delivered files of the channel running on the Mac (HLS 3 playlists, DASH with the playout's own loop Periods): valid. `edge-variants` 62/62 (two checks now say what the default is), `edge-rights` 38/38, `edge-viewas` 5/5, `docs` ok (routes and OpenAPI regenerated for the three routes). Full `node scripts/check.js`: **197/201 suites, 6133 assertions**; the four others fail identically on the tree before this change (`fast` clock dependent, `live-degraded` 12/17, `live-record` needs volt-web's node_modules) or pass alone (`recordings` 35/35). volt-app typecheck clean. **Left:** a programme that names its own replacement asset still gets the stream's loop (a package per replacement asset is the follow-up); the function's `cf.selectRequestOriginById` must be confirmed on the first apply; the two-tab check on the Mac (`/live/<id>/master.m3u8?as=AU` and `?as=MY` side by side) needs a real player, the container's Chromium has no H.264; Multiview's view-as still uses `_rp` (1.14c). | 1.14d | **CMCD v2 in the Volt SDK** (rewritten 24 Oct, see QOE_REPORTING.md sections 2 to 4 and 10). Request mode (query keys) on every engine; event mode (batched POST, `application/cmcd`) to `POST /api/cmcd`; the play attempt opened before entitlement, DRM or the manifest; custom keys for the startup spans, app and SDK version, device model, decoder, DRM outcome; `x-amz-cf-id` exposed and copied into `rr` events; CloudFront cache policy ignores CMCD keys; `sid` = the play gate's playback session | done (no release yet, 24 Oct 2026) | Player test: request keys valid for CMCD v2; event reports carry `v`, `ts`, `e`, `sid`, `sn`; a refused entitlement and a failed licence still produce a view with the right code; two sessions share one cached segment |  **Built.** **SDK** (`volt-player/src/sdk/cmcd.ts`, encoding by the SVTA Common Media Library `@svta/cml-cmcd` 2.8.0, about 20 KB minified on a 1 MB bundle): a **play attempt** opens when an item starts loading, before the play gate is asked (preloading `d` until play is pressed when not autoplay, so startup is timed from the press); its `sid` is a UUID made by the player, sent to the gate (`POST /api/availability` `sid`, now a claim in the playback token) and used as the Analytics beacon session, so gate, beacons, CDN rows and reports join on one id. **Request mode**: a Shaka request filter adds `CMCD=` to every playlist, init and segment request (v=2, sid, cid, ot m/i/v/a/c, sf, st, su until the first frame, bs after a stall, br and tb from the stream or the playing variant, bl, mtp, d where Shaka exposes it); licence and key requests get none; native Safari HLS and progressive files carry it on the first URL (the browser fetches the rest). **Event mode** to `POST /api/cmcd` (`?appKey=`, also `/api/public/cmcd`): play states s/d/p/k/r/a/e/f/q (seeking is not a stall), `e` with `ec` and a `com.volt-step` (entitlement, manifest, drm, segment, decode, playback), `bc`, `t` every 30 s (bl, mtp, pb, tpb, dfa, ltc), `b`, `pr`, `m`/`um`, and `rr` for the startup requests, 1 in 50 after, every failed and every slow (2 s) one, with `ttfb`, `ttlb`, the URL without its CMCD and the CDN's `x-amz-cf-id` as `com.volt-cfid`; every report carries v, ts, e, sid, sn. First frame is the first presented frame (`requestVideoFrameCallback`), with `msd` and custom spans (`com.volt-tent`, `tman`, `tdrm`, `tseg`, `tdec`, `toth`) that add up to `msd`; app, app version, SDK version and device model on the first report. Transport (`CmcdSender`): batches of 10 or 5 s, 429 doubles the batch and waits (Retry-After), 410 stops the session, 5xx and network retried with back-off, kept while offline (newest 1000), keepalive on page hide. New player options `cmcd` (off, app name and version) and `drm` (licence servers, licence headers); `player.sessionId`. **Collector** (`modules/cmcd.ts`, `services/qoe/cmcdParse.ts` our own CMCD reader, `services/qoe/rawStore.ts`): verifies the publishable key, keeps every valid line raw first (`QOE_RAW_DIR`, NDJSON per account, day and hour, no IP), 204, 400, 410 (unknown or secret key), 413 (over 200 lines), 415, 429 (600 a minute per session). **Terraform**: the delivery cache policies already ignore query strings, so CMCD never splits the cache (now said in a comment and tested); new `aws_cloudfront_response_headers_policy.delivery` on all four delivery behaviours exposes `x-amz-cf-id`, `x-amz-cf-pop`, `x-cache`, `age`, `cmsd-static`, `cmsd-dynamic` and sends `Timing-Allow-Origin: *`; the load balancer routes `/api/cmcd` to the public tier; `terraform validate` passes (1.9.8). **Tests:** new `test/cmcd.check.ts` **73/73** (every request kind and every report checked with the library's own CMCD v2 validator; sn without gaps; spans adding up to msd; a refusal and a failed licence before the first frame still a view with step and code; the transport rules; our parser equal to the library's decoder on every line; the collector's codes and raw store; the token sid; the Terraform rules). New `test/cmcd-browser.check.ts` **34/34** in Chromium through the real gate and collector from a stand-in CDN on a third origin: 15 of 15 media requests carried valid v2 CMCD; 12 event reports, sn 1 to 12, valid `application/cmcd` POSTs; first frame 228 ms (entitlement 97, manifest 14, first segment 14, decode 65, other 38); the 8 startup `rr` reports carry the CDN's own request id read across origins; token sid and `play_sessions.session_key` equal the CMCD sid; a second viewer's different CMCD on the same segment URLs and each segment fetched from the origin once; a refused entitlement (site not on the app's list) reported as gate-403 at the entitlement step with no media request; an encrypted DASH asset whose licence server answers 500 reported as shaka-6007 at the DRM step, never a first frame, the licence POST without CMCD. Against the SDK before this change: 15/34. Full `node scripts/check.js`: **199/204 suites, 6353 assertions, 23 failed**: fast 44/45 (clock dependent, as before), live-degraded 12/17 and xmltv-import 34/35 (fail the same on the tree before, as recorded at 1.14c), recordings 19/35 in the full run and 35/35 alone, player-stats-browser 23/25 the same with this change taken out (the uncommitted Debug mode rename in `stats.ts` and `strings.ts` from another session). Neighbours: player-overlay-browser 31/31, player-sdk 47/47, player-sdk-api 29/29, player-api 32/32, apps 8/8, edge-rights 38/38, rights-preview 50/50, hypermedia 57/57, auth-boundary 23/23, custom-role 31/31, docs ok. Typechecks clean on the device (volt-app, volt-web, volt-player). API reference: overlay entries and the Cmcd service statement, `docs:routes`, `docs:openapi` regenerated. **Not yet:** view rows, minute aggregates and the reports (1.14e); the old beacon path still runs beside it; Shaka's compiled build hides segment end times, so `d` is sent only where Shaka exposes it; CMSD (2.10); hls.js and dash.js used directly by a customer are not wrapped (they have their own CMCD settings); not run on the Mac (there: `DATABASE_URL=... npx tsx test/cmcd-browser.check.ts` with `CHROMIUM_PATH` set to Chrome). **Design note:** QOE_REPORTING.md says the play gate issues the session id; the gate's anonymous answer is edge cached, so the player makes the id and tells the gate, which is what principle 2 needs anyway. Custom key values travel as strings (CTA-5004-B), so spans read `"120"`. Not released: other sessions' uncommitted CHANGELOG edits and change notes are in the tree. |
| 1.14e | **QoE collector and the first reports** (QOE_REPORTING.md). Collector writes raw batches first (local folder on the Mac, S3 Parquet in the cloud, Object Lock and the 30 d / 90 d / Deep Archive lifecycle), then one view row per view and minute DDSketch aggregates into a **separate analytics database** with its own pool; replay command (idempotent, by day partition, definitions version stamped). Reports 1 playback success, 2 startup, 3 buffering, 4 quality and ABR, 10 engagement, 12 telemetry health, filterable by content, live/VOD, device model, OS/browser, app and player version, codec, CDN, region, ISP/network | done (no release yet, 24 Oct 2026) | Simulated viewers with planted stalls, failures and switches give the expected p50/p95/p99 and affected-viewer counts; a planted bad app release is isolated by its filter; deleting derived rows and replaying gives identical reports; the analytics database stopped mid-run loses nothing and stream starts are unaffected | **Built.** **Live path** (`services/qoe/collector.ts`): `modules/cmcd.ts` keeps each batch raw first, then hands it over; each session's state in memory, built by the same `ViewBuilder` a replay runs (`services/qoe/views.ts`, the section 4 definitions written down as version 1); every 10 s one batched write of the closed views (quit, fatal, 60 s silence; a view resumed within 60 minutes is rewritten, same id), the minute aggregates (counts, sums and DDSketch startup sketches, `sketch.ts`, 1 % error, merged by adding bins) and the collector's own accepted and refused counts. **Separate analytics database** (`analyticsDb.ts`): `ANALYTICS_DATABASE_URL`, else `<db>_qoe` on the same server created on first use, its own pool (4) and 10 s statement timeout; the control plane's pool is never touched. If it is down, writes are held (up to 200 000 views) and retried, players still get 204. **Raw store** (`rawStore.ts`): the request's context on each session's first row (user agent and platform hints, country, region and ASN from CloudFront headers when the edge is trusted, never the IP); in the cloud `QOE_RAW_BUCKET` gzipped NDJSON objects per account, day and hour every 10 s or 64 MB (not Parquet yet). **Replay** (`replay.ts`, `src/tools/qoe-replay.ts --day`, `POST /api/analytics/qoe/replay`): reads the day and its neighbours, replaces the day's views and minutes in one transaction, idempotent. **Reports** (`reports.ts`, `modules/qoe.ts` under `/api/analytics/qoe`): overview (reports 1, 2, 3, 4, 10, 12), series, breakdown (worst segments), dimensions, definitions, collector; 18 filters; percentiles nearest rank with views and affected sessions. **Console**: Analytics > Playback (`volt-web/src/pages/PlaybackReports.tsx`). **Terraform** `deploy/terraform/qoe_raw.tf`: versioned bucket, Object Lock governance (90 d), 30 d Glacier IR, 90 d Deep Archive, expiry `qoe_raw_retention_days` 760; task role and env on every control-plane role; `terraform validate` ok (1.9.8). **Tests:** new `test/qoe-reports.check.ts` **71/71**: 129 simulated viewers through the real collector give exactly the planned attempts, failures, EBFF, failure after start, startup p50/p95/p99 1000/1490/4000, rebuffering 70 000 ms over 6 159 000 ms, 10 switches and 5 oscillations, time-weighted 2979 kbps, event loss, late and refused reports; the planted bad release 2.1.0 isolated by app version, device model, OS and country, worst in the breakdown at 50 %; sketch percentiles within 1 %; deleting the derived rows and replaying gives byte-identical reports, twice; with the analytics database refusing connections reports are still taken (30/30 204), the play gate answers in 18 ms, nothing is lost once it is back and live equals replay; S3 raw path with a stand-in client; the Terraform rules. Browser (Chromium, real API and Vite, 160 seeded viewers): all eight panels render, the app version filter narrows to 2.1.0 (20 attempts, 50 % failures), worst segments lists 2.1.0 first, no sideways scroll at 1440 and 390 px, no console errors. Steps: preflight, setup, sources, channel (failover, auto failback, slate on total loss, recording, captions), start (segments, the decoded frame is RED), air, captions (SUBTITLES, the cue playlist grows, a cue has words), rights (a programme blocking AU with the loop: preview AU replace and MY allow, AU's rendition carries `/filler/` segments and MY's none, in the preview play address and on the public address with `?as=`), failover (BLUE, slate, RED again, read from delivered frames), stop, record (30 s or more, the slate marked over the hole, no loop segment in the recording), clip (20 s, build job), vod (its master plays, ffprobe about 20 s), publish, radio (synthetic ICY source, on air, a source title in now playing), event (live, RED, recording), FAST (run order of the clip, prepared, public, its playlist airs the clip). `TESTING_WORKFLOWS.md` section 8 rewritten as the run sheet (a row per step, settings), `docs/engineering/running-on-the-mac.md` points to it. **Tests:** new `volt-app/test/e2e-runsheet.check.ts` **14/14** (plan and step order, a doc row per step, every one of the script's 46 API calls and 6 cleanup routes is a route in `docs/api-routes.json`, then a real API on a fresh account runs setup, sources, channel and radio for real and `--cleanup` leaves nothing; the route check fails on a renamed route). (c), (d) and the rest of (e) split to 3.2-2) | Check suite: index locates frames across segment boundaries, an exact frame matches a ground-truth decode, frames cached; smart render output equals a full render frame-for-frame on at least three clips (else stays off, recorded why); workbench frame stepping checked in a browser | **(a) Frame index for assets** (`volt-app/src/services/frames/`): `fmp4.ts` reads each CMAF segment's fragment boxes (tfhd, tfdt, trun: sample count, durations, composition offsets, sync flag) so a segment's frames are counted with no ffprobe and no decode; MPEG-TS falls back to one ffprobe packet scan. `frameIndex.ts` (pure): the rendition to index is the tallest video variant of the master, per segment its first frame and frame count, the rate from the commonest sample duration (25/1, 30000/1001), binary-search `locateFrame`, `frameAtMs`, and `HH:MM:SS:FF` timecode with SMPTE drop-frame at 29.97 and 59.94 both ways. Stored per asset in the new `asset_frame_indexes` table (migration 0121; its own table because library lists select `v.*`), made by a new `frames.index` job queued after every `clip.extract` and `vod.encode`, by `GET /api/vod/{id}/frame-index` when missing (`build=true` makes it now), and by a leader sweep every 10 minutes for older media. **Recordings get no stored index, on purpose:** the per-run segment scan (`localRecordingIndex.ts`) already places every segment on the wall clock from its tfdt, and the frame inside comes from that one segment's boxes on request; storing a second index of a 24x7 channel's 21,600 segments a day would cost writes for nothing (cost rule). **(b) Exact frame on request:** `GET /api/vod/{id}/frame` (`n`, `tc` or `ms`) and `GET /api/recordings/{id}/frame` (`n`, `ms` or wall clock `at`) read only the segment holding the frame (with its init), decode forward and keep the k-th picture, refusing a segment that does not open on a keyframe rather than returning a neighbour. The still (JPEG q2, source size) goes to the image store under `derived/<account>/frames/` (asset stills under the index build time, so a re-encode never serves an old frame), made once per frame even when asked for at the same moment, recorded in `frame_stills` and pruned by the sweep after `FRAME_STILL_TTL_DAYS` (7). The answer carries the frame number, timecode, time, the segment and frame inside it, and a signed image-service URL at `w` (default 640, every width cached there); `redirect=true` answers 302 to the image. Under `/api/vod` and `/api/recordings` so the permission map gives Media library: view and Recordings: view. API reference (overlay) and a developer recipe (Show the exact frame at a timecode) updated; no console change yet, so no user guide change. **Checked:** typechecks clean on the device (volt-app, volt-web). | 1.7b | **Song metadata in the manifest, read at the playhead** (Christian, 3 Nov: DVR must show what was playing where the viewer is, not what is playing now). Each song written into the radio playlists as an `EXT-X-DATERANGE` (HLS) and a DASH `EventStream` event, with start, end, title, artist, album, artwork URL and Listen link; the artwork added as a new attribute once found (allowed by the HLS rules: values already written never change). The Volt player SDK works out now playing, up next and history from these at the playhead (as it already does for programmes) and stops polling the now-playing API; the API stays for websites, apps and partners. At the live edge the next song is not known, so Up next names the next programme from the guide there. | D2 | **Migration safety check** (Christian, 3 Nov: updates without downtime). Rolling deploys run new and old tasks side by side, so a release may only add: new tables, nullable columns or columns with defaults, indexes built concurrently. Dropping or renaming a column or table, changing a type, or adding NOT NULL without a default is refused unless an earlier release already stopped using it, declared with `-- volt:contract <what> after v0.60.0`. Runs in `scripts/release/check.mjs` and in D1. | H2b | **Watchfolder defaults by type, companions by name, and images and audio from a site** (Christian, 5 Nov). (a) **Built-in default rules** for every new watchfolder (local, bucket and site), editable and each switchable off, shown as ordinary rules in the console and the dry run; a file nothing recognises still goes to review. Images (png, jpg, jpeg, webp, gif, tif, tiff) to the Image Library; video (mp4, mov, m4v, mxf, mkv, ts, webm) to the Media Library; audio-only (mp3, wav, m4a, aac, flac) to the Media Library as an **audio asset** encoded to an audio-only ladder and played with the audio skin (check what the encode and player already support; split if audio assets are not there yet); captions (SRT, WebVTT, TTML/DFXP) and metadata sidecars (JSON, XML/NFO, CSV), up to 25 MB each, become **companion files** (`services/companions`, the existing upload limit). Existing folders keep their rules; an action in the console adds the defaults to one. (b) **Companions matched by name:** a caption or sidecar attaches to the asset whose source file name (without extension) its own name starts with, longest match wins: `Scrubs-s1-e1_en.vtt` goes with `Scrubs-s1-e1.mp4`, language from the rest of the name (`nameHints`: `en`, `episode.fr.srt`, `.sdh`, `.forced`). Matched in the same folder, or to an asset already in the library from that folder. One that arrives first waits (Waiting, "waiting for its video") up to **24 hours**, attaches as soon as the video lands, otherwise goes to Needs review with why. Filename metadata as today (`parseFilename`): `Scrubs-s1-e1.mp4` is series Scrubs, season 1, episode 1, title "Episode 1 (S1)", shown as "Scrubs Episode 1 (S1)", with English subtitles from the companion. (c) **From a site:** images, audio and companions uploaded by the agent too (H2 refuses images); images land through the same image ingest as a local drop, companions through the companion store, all on the site tus path kept on the item. (d) **Where a custom rule may send each type** (Christian, 5 Nov): an image to the Image Library or to Loops & Fillers as a **slate**; a video to the Media Library or to Loops & Fillers as a **loop** (the `loop` target kind already does this for video; a slate image to Loops & Fillers is new, today `slate` files an image in the Image Library). The rule form offers the destination by the file's type, so a rule cannot send an image to the Media Library or a caption to Loops & Fillers. (e) **Image type from the file name** (Christian, 5 Nov), for bulk ingest: a default rule reads a type word at the end of an image's name, after `-`, `_`, `.` or a space, ignoring case: `Scrubs-s1-e1-thumbnail.jpg`, `Scrubs-s1-e1_poster.png`. Words map to the account's asset image types (`asset_image_types`, standard set in `services/media/assetImageRules.ts`): thumbnail or thumb to Thumbnail; poster to Poster; cover to Cover; hero, backdrop, background or fanart to Hero backdrop; banner to Banner; social or share to Social share card; title, titleart, clearlogo or logo-title to Title art; an account's own type by its key or label. The stem before the word is matched to an asset like a companion (longest prefix, same folder or already in the library from it, waits up to 24 h): the image goes into the Image Library and becomes that asset's image of that type (`vod_asset_images`; poster or the type's primary rule sets `poster_key` as the asset page does), with the existing fit warnings (aspect, minimum size) shown, never refused. With no asset after 24 h it stays in the Image Library with the matching library kind (poster to poster, thumbnail to keyframe, logo or brand to brand) and the item says no asset matched. Standalone words with no stem, such as `brand`, `logo`, `watermark` and `slate` at the end of a name, set the library kind as today's rule targets do. An image name with no type word goes to the Image Library as an image, as (a). **Otherwise, an image type on the rule** (Christian, 5 Nov): any accepting rule for images may also name an image type (the account's types, offered as a picker in the rule form and in the API as `imageType`), so a folder whose names carry no type word, or use the house's own words (`*_key_art.jpg`), still sets the type; a type word in the name, when present, wins over the rule's type only if the rule says so (`imageTypeFromName`, on by default). The dry run shows the type a file would get and the asset it would attach to. (d) A lease reaped on the last attempt also fails the asset with the reason. | done (v0.66.0, 8 Nov 2026; (a) the agent worker and (d) the reaper. (b) the placement, (c) the bucket on AWS and the Mac Docker runs split to H4-3) | Mac run: a site worker in Docker encodes a file dropped on site, it plays from the local origin, the source sits in the local archive. It leases `site.vod-probe` and `site.vod-encode`, runs `vod_encoder.py` with the cloud's settings and only its own paths: in the pinned image under Docker when `VOLT_ENCODER_IMAGE` is set (source folder read only, scratch as /work, `--network none`, `--gpus all` only for a job planned for NVENC), else from `VOLT_ENCODER_BIN` with the host's python3. Progress every few seconds from the encoder's own progress lines and during upload keeps the lease; a lost lease kills the encoder and nothing more is sent. The package is declared in parts (1000 per request), each file uploaded through its own URL with no credential on the request (a presigned bucket URL would refuse one), then completed; a refused package is left to the cloud's retry. The attempt's scratch folder is removed when the job ends either way. A missing file fails for good (`source_missing`), a missing folder is a retry (`source_unavailable`, share not mounted), an encoder with no answer is a retry (`encoder_failed`). GPU jobs only to GPU hosts: the lease skips a job whose encoder settings say `gpu` for a host that reported no NVIDIA GPU (`claimInPool` option), and a GPU job that reaches a host without one is handed back (`no_gpu`), never encoded on the CPU. (d) `reapExpiredLeases` calls `siteEncodeAttemptFailed` for site encode jobs: the half declaration and staging upload are dropped, and on the last attempt the asset fails with the reason. The URL signs the file's SHA-256 as `x-amz-checksum-sha256` for an agent that says it sends signed headers (`signedHeaders: true`, agent 0.9.0), so S3 refuses other bytes; an older agent gets a host-only URL. ## Phase 2: AWS, Terraform and the cost functions

| # | Item | Status | Acceptance and validation | Result |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2.1 | **Terraform ready to apply.** `terraform fmt -check`, `terraform validate`, tflint if obtainable; variables documented in `terraform.tfvars.example`; a `deploy/terraform/APPLY.md` runbook for the first apply | done (no release yet, 27 Oct 2026) | Validate passes (or the exact reason it cannot run here is recorded); every variable the app reads at runtime is wired from Terraform (compare `src/config.ts` env vars against the task definitions) | **Built.** `terraform fmt -check` clean (two lines in `ecs_cluster.tf` reformatted), `terraform init -backend=false` and `terraform validate` pass (Terraform 1.13.4, AWS provider 6.57.1 from the lock file; `deploy/terraform-docs` validates too); tflint not obtainable here (its GitHub releases are not reachable from the container), recorded in APPLY.md. **Env wiring:** new `scripts/deploy/check-terraform-env.mjs` reads every setting the code under `volt-app/src` reads (278) against what the control-plane task definitions set (75) and `deploy/terraform/env-coverage.json` (203 with a class and reason: 141 default, 22 local, 17 operator, 23 not-on-aws-yet), and also fails on a secret reference to a missing key, a setting with the wrong value for AWS, and a variable with no line in the example. It found and this item wired: `MEDIA_STORE_DRIVER=s3` and `INGEST_DESTINATION=s3` (media and uploads were going to a Fargate task's disk), `INGEST_S3_ACCELERATE` with the bucket's acceleration and a PUT CORS rule for the console's origin (presigned uploads would have failed), `MEDIA_ARCHIVE_ENABLED`, `VOLT_DEPLOYMENT`, a generated `SOCIAL_TOKEN_KEY` (was derived from the session secret), `s3:RestoreObject` for the storage ladder; the operator's own settings through `app_env` and `app_secret_keys` (in the integrations secret), refused when they would set a name twice. Also: the origin bucket is no longer force-emptied by a prod destroy (README said it was not), README step 4 no longer tells you to apply only `0001_init.sql`. `terraform.tfvars.example` now lists all 90 variables. `deploy/terraform/APPLY.md` is the first-apply runbook; `docs/engineering/deploying-to-aws.md` points to it and the check. **Tests:** new `volt-app/test/terraform-env.check.ts` **43/43** (container through check.js, and on the device); with this item's settings taken out of `ecs_services.tf` it reports the media store, uploads and the social key; on the Terraform before this item the checker reports 59 problems. engineering-guides 35/35; volt-app typecheck clean; volt-web only the three known errors in another session's files. **Found, queued:** 2.1a (control-plane state on the task's disk, no ffmpeg/packager/Python in its image) and 2.1b (radio and live captions on AWS); email provider as needs input. |
| 2.1a | **Control-plane state and tools on AWS** (found by 2.1; see `deploy/terraform/env-coverage.json`, class not-on-aws-yet, item 2.1a). On Fargate the control plane keeps work on its own disk: uploaded slates and loops (`FILLER_DIR`, served from `/filler`), prebuilt loop segments and the failover control file (`ORIGIN_DIR`), clip, VOD encode and ad conditioning work and downloads (`CLIPS_DIR`) and the trash (`VOLT_TRASH_DIR`). A replaced task loses them and the other tasks never had them. And in-process jobs (clip extract, VOD encode, loop preparation, intro detection) run ffmpeg, ffprobe, the packager and Python, which the control-plane image (`volt-app/Dockerfile`) does not carry. Per piece: move it to the bucket, or run the job as its own ECS task (the vod-extractor and thumbnailer already do), and reclassify its settings. If the choice needs Christian (for example an EFS mount against moving to S3, on cost), ask rather than pick  **Decided (Christian, 27 Oct): option B, S3.** Keep files in the bucket (slates, loops, prebuilt loop segments, VOD outputs, downloads, the trash, images, logs), because that is where users reach them from; the task's own disk only for in-flight scratch work that a job reads and writes while it runs. If a piece shows a real performance, latency or cost problem on S3, stop and say so with the numbers rather than moving it to disk. The jobs that need ffmpeg, the packager or Python run as their own ECS tasks (like the vod-extractor and thumbnailer), so the control-plane image stays lean. | 2.4a | **Projected load learns from what runs, and counts storage** (Christian, 27 Oct: calculate CPU, hardware, bandwidth and storage before allocation from the bitrates and number of renditions, and gain experience). Today `fleet/streamLoad.ts` estimates CPU, memory, GPU share and network from fixed constants and `calibrate()` corrects only a signature that has already run 3 times for 2 hours, so a new ladder is always sized from the constants and storage is not projected at all. Build: (a) fit the per-component coefficients (CPU per rendition by codec, resolution, frame rate and bitrate; packaging and DRM cost; mux overhead on bandwidth) from observed runs across all streams, per instance family, with a floor and a bound so one odd run cannot move them far; (b) project storage: scratch disk a task needs while it runs, and S3 written per hour and held under the retention rules, from the bitrates; (c) a per-class report of projection against actual (CPU, network, storage) so the error is visible; (d) placement refuses a server whose task disk would run short | done (no release yet, 27 Oct 2026) | Unit tests on the fit (known synthetic runs recover their coefficients; outliers bounded); placement-planner and placement-e2e green; a seeded history where a new ladder's first estimate moves toward the observed cost; storage projection for a known ladder within 10 % of the bitrate arithmetic | Built: learned multipliers per component (codec, decode, packaging, ingest, audio, DRM...) and instance family from every solo-measured run, pulled to 1 by a 3-run prior, outliers set aside (4 MADs, repeated), clamped 0.5x to 2x; applied before per-signature calibration (basis `fitted`, `FLEET_LEARN`); storage projected per hour (task disk, S3, held under retention) and stored at launch; per-class projection against actual for CPU, network and storage in `GET /api/platform/fleet-plan`; placement refuses a server whose task disk would run short within `FLEET_DISK_HORIZON_HOURS`. load-fit 47/47, load-learning 22/22 (a never-run ladder within 5 % of its real cost through `assignStream`, constants over 20 % off; storage within 10 % of the bitrate arithmetic), placement-planner 26/26, placement-e2e 21/21, fleet suites 13/13. | 2.4c | **The encoder's task disk on AWS** (found by 2.4a). The packager keeps the whole run on the task disk (`--preserved_segments_outside_live_window 0` in `volt-encoder/bin/encoder.py`) while `s3-uploader.sh` mirrors it to S3, and the launch templates set no root volume, so a 4-rung 1080p ladder fills the AMI's 30 GiB in about six hours on any server. On AWS keep only a short window locally (S3 holds the run; the sync never deletes remote objects), set the encoder hosts' root volume in `ecs_cluster.tf` and `ecs_capacity_*.tf`, then set `FLEET_TASK_DISK_GIB` so placement's disk check (2.4a) is on. The Mac's local runs are the recordings and stay as they are | done (no release yet, 27 Oct 2026) | Encoder test: on an S3 output, local segments older than the window are removed and every one of them reached S3 first; a local output keeps every run; terraform validate; terraform-env check with `FLEET_TASK_DISK_GIB` set; load-learning disk checks still green | Built without touching `encoder.py` (another session's uncommitted work): `s3-uploader.sh` keeps `LOCAL_KEEP_SECONDS` (Terraform `encoder_local_keep_seconds`, 1800, never under DVR plus 2 min) and removes numbered segments only after a clean sync that started after they were written; init, manifests and the caption file stay; S3 keeps the whole run. gp3 encrypted roots (`encoder_root_volume_gib` 30, `gpu_root_volume_gib` 60), stopped tasks release disk after 15 min, `FLEET_TASK_DISK_GIB` and `ENCODER_LOCAL_KEEP_SECONDS` set, and the disk projection caps each stream at its window (`scratchCapMib`). encoder-task-disk 42/42 (uploader and projection cases fail on HEAD), load-learning 23/23, load-fit 47/47, aws-radio-captions 54/54, 8 fleet neighbours green, terraform fmt and validate clean; terraform-env 47/47 with another session's new `FAST_LAUNCHER` read set aside (it fails on that alone, before and after). Pre-existing: captions-gpu-sharing stops at its database part on a fresh template (instances need a channel or event), same on HEAD. Not run on real AWS |

At 1 M viewer hours a month on two vCPUs the reports answer a day in under 0.1 s and 31 days in under 0.5 s, but 13 months takes 0.7 to 6.1 s: exact nearest-rank percentiles and exact distinct sessions over 16 M views are the cost (DDSketch estimates still took 1.4 s for the startup report). Ways to get under 1 s: (A) daily rollups with mergeable sketches for windows over 31 days, estimates within about 1 % (like the trend lines already are) and only the minute dimensions filterable at that range; (B) exact everywhere on bigger replicas (r7g.2xlarge, four times the cores, about $460 a month more for the pair; likely still over 1 s for the widest reports, not measured); (C) accept seconds for windows over 90 days | needs input: which of A, B or C for windows longer than 31 days? | Under 1 s for every report over 13 months at the modelled size, numbers from `scripts/qoe-ch-bench.ts` | |
| 2.11b | **Collector sessions routed by sid across tasks** (found by 2.11). With two or more collector tasks behind the load balancer a session's batches land on different tasks, so live view rows of such sessions stay provisional until the day is replayed (collector.ts header). Route by sid (a hash of the sid to a task, forwarding a batch that arrived at the wrong one) and replay yesterday each night | done (no release yet, 28 Oct 2026) | Two collector processes, batches of one session sent to both: one view row, identical to a replay; memory per task falls with the task count | Tasks that take reports list themselves in the analytics database (`qoe_collectors`, heartbeat 5 s) and pick each session's owner by rendezvous hashing of the sid (`services/qoe/sidRoute.ts`); the receiving task keeps the batch raw, derives what it owns and forwards the rest every 200 ms, one signed request per peer (`POST /api/cmcd/_peer`); a failed forward is derived where it arrived. Nightly replay of yesterday after 02:30 UTC, exactly once across tasks (`services/qoe/nightly.ts`). Terraform: control-plane tasks reach each other on 8080, `QOE_ROUTE=auto`. New qoe-sid-route **29/29** (three runs): two real processes, 40 sessions sent to both in turn; without routing 40/40 live rows differ from a replay and each process holds 40 sessions, with routing every live row identical to the replay, 20 + 20 sessions held, unsigned or stale forwards 401, a killed owner's forwards derived on the survivor, nightly replay once. qoe-reports 71/71, qoe-reports-more 70/70, cmcd 73/73, journey 68/68, cdn-logs 68/68, delivery-guidance 72/72, stream-report 77/77 (9/9 suites, 499 assertions); terraform fmt, validate, test 6/6; typecheck clean; engineering-guides 35/35. Not run on real ECS tasks; forwarding CPU under load not measured. |

| 3.0 | **Metadata from the file name.** Added 29 Oct (Christian), placed first in Phase 3 because it smooths every ingest path. Read the original file name as a metadata source after probe and ID3: recognise resolution, content type or kind (a Kind value sets its parent type too), year and date, season and episode, part, language, codec and version tokens, record each as a found value under `metadata.ingest.filename`, strip it, and title-case what is left as the title (or the series name for episodes, with the title from a template, default `Episode {episode} (S{season})`). Precedence, account file name rules, the manual **Read from file name** trigger (asset and bulk, preview with ticks, never overwriting a field a person edited) and worked examples are in `volt-app/docs/DESIGN_filename_metadata.md` | done (no release yet, 29 Oct 2026; built in a session with Christian, widened to classification rules, related-content links and the API include) | The parser test passes every example in the design (`7th-voyage-of-sinbad_1958_trailer_1080p.webm` gives Trailer / promo, Kind Trailer, year 1958, resolution 1080p, title 7th Voyage Of Sinbad; `bluey_s1_ep01.mp4` gives an episode titled Episode 1 (S1)) plus real names from the demo assets and watch folder fixtures; ingest suite: an ID3 title beats the file name, a rule's explicit type beats it, the probe's resolution beats it and a mismatch is recorded; re-run leaves an edited field alone unless ticked; the rule switch, the account rules and the test box work in Admin > Ingest; API docs regenerated | Parser, IF/THEN classification rules with precedence (person > ingest rule > account rule > built-in) and conflicts, fields and title from the name, content-link field replacing the free-text Trailer for (table content_links, migration 0117 moves old values: one exact title links by id, anything else stays a name), automatic linking only on one clear match with suggestions otherwise, re-matching when a record arrives, `GET /api/vod/{id}?include=linkedContent` (one level, both directions, account only), console: link field, Read from file name (asset and bulk), Related content, File names card in Ingest Rules. ## Set aside

| Item | Status |
|---|---|
| Automatic now-playing / EPG detection (guide feeds, fingerprinting, transcript) | needs input: which route first (broadcaster guide adapters, a fingerprint vendor and its cost, or transcript only), and for which stations |
| TAMS read-side API | needs input: is TAMS interoperability needed for a customer or partner now, or later |
| SportsML-G2 ingest and typed SportsTeam entities | needs input: extend `target_kind` beyond streamable things (a decision on the entity model) |
| Loop origin on channels | skipped: a loop is not a kind of stream. Christian, 22 Oct: a loop is a filler video asset of any length in Loops & Fillers, used to replace content when the source drops or a rights window blocks it; it repeats to fill the gap (a 5 minute loop fills a 20 minute blackout) and is cut at the end of the window. See items 1.12 and 2.5 |
| API docs for the six new library routes | done before the queue (regenerated 22 Oct; `docs.check.sh` covers it) |
| Axinom DRM and FairPlay playback | needs input: Axinom tenant id and key; Apple FairPlay deployment package and licence endpoint |
| Social publishing | needs input: developer app registrations |
| AWS first apply and Tier-3 real run | needs input: AWS account, credentials and region for the Tier-2 deployment |
| Deleting an event (from 1.14-G1) | needs input: the Events list asks "Delete event? Its recording (if any) stays in the Recordings library", but deleting an event removes its recordings with it (and a full-event replay made from it), as the event page says. Which is intended: keep the recording and replay when an event is deleted, or remove them? Either way the two messages become one, and the delete should list what goes, as channels do |
| Caption offset rules (from 1.14-G2) | needs input: Admin > Captions sets **Hold captions back by** in whole seconds from 0 to 60 ("the playout delay that keeps words in step"), while a channel's Settings > Subtitles sets **Subtitle offset** from -30 to 30 with decimals ("positive shows them later, negative earlier"). Both write the same output profile value, which is stored in whole seconds. Which is right: may the offset be negative or fractional, and what range? The two screens then get one rule and one wording |
| Who gets which rights class (from 1.14a) | needs input: the edge and the Mac honour a signed rights class (`vrc`) and programmes can exempt classes (`metadata.rights_exempt`), but nothing issues a class yet. Should the play gate give a viewer a class from a client group, from an entitlement product, or from the app, and what are the class names (for example `premium`)? Also: should the guide editor show a field for the exempt classes? |
| Payment provider secrets (from 1.14-G4) | needs input: Payments asks each account for a **Secret reference** to its Stripe, App Store or Google Play secrets "in the secrets store", which only whoever runs the deployment can fill. In the multi-tenant service, should customers paste their secret keys into Volt (stored encrypted, like social tokens), or does Volt staff load them per account? |
| Replacement content at the edge (from 1.14-G5) | needs input: a programme's **Replacement for this period** names a slate, loop or VOD asset, but viewers in a replaced window are given the stream's own filler loop (built from the channel's Filler on total loss), whatever the slot names, and a slot with no country list has no effect. The guides now describe that. Should the variant service play the slot's own replacement instead (a loop per replacement asset), or should the slot's replacement picker go? |
| Members-only and premium on the public live address (from 1.14-G5) | needs input: the play gate checks sign-in, purchases and playback tokens, but the public live address (`/live/<id>/master.m3u8`) checks only on-air status, geo and programme rights, so anyone with the address can play members-only or premium content where geo allows. The Securing a live stream guide tells customers to deliver such content through the player and their apps. Should the public address require a playback token for streams whose access rules are not free (and at the edge too)? |
| Auto-publish for uploads and watch folder files (from 1.14-G5a) | needs input: auto-publish now runs for clips (when built, or when approved if the rule requires a review) and full-event replays. Should a finished upload or watch folder file publish itself too when its rule has Auto-publish (after its encode, or on approval)? Today it never does, and the guides say so |
| Catch-up for ad-free viewers (from 1.14k-3) | needs input: a replay of a live recording now leaves out the broadcast's ad breaks for viewers who get on-demand ads, but viewers who play without ads (they paid, or the rule has no mid-rolls) still get it as recorded, broadcast ads included, and their title is longer by those breaks (so a bookmark moves between the two). Should ad-free viewers get the broadcast breaks cut out too? |
| Event Manager role (from 1.14-G5a (m)) | needs input: the default Event Manager role cannot open the embed player, use an event's Access tab or approve in Review. Should it be able to do any of these by default? |
| Email on AWS (from 2.1) | needs input: production sends invites, password resets, sign-in codes and alerts only when `SMTP_URL` and `NOTIFY_FROM` are set. Which provider (Amazon SES through its SMTP endpoint, or another), and which sender address and domain? Terraform takes them through `app_secret_keys` and `app_env` (APPLY.md step 7); SES would also need the domain verified and production access requested. |
| Who may hold the image signing key (from 1.14-G3a (k)) | needs input: `GET /api/images/signing-key` answers any signed-in admin user, Viewer tier included, because the console signs every thumbnail with it in the browser (`imageSigning.ts`). The key renders any image of the account and cannot be taken back without rotating the server secret. Which do you want: (A) keep it as it is (anyone who can sign in may render the account's images, which they can already see); (B) only roles with Apps, API keys & players (or Files, uploads & storage: Manage) get the key, and everyone else's console asks the server to sign thumbnails in batches (`POST /api/images/sign`, slower first paint); or (C) the console gets a short-lived signing key per session and the account key is kept for developers? |
